<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> For this protein, which specialized cochlear hair-cell site is the more specific cellular-component location?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> The kinocilium tip<br><br><strong>B.</strong> The hair-cell synaptic ribbon<br><br><strong>C.</strong> The stereocilium tip<br><br><strong>D.</strong> The hair-cell cuticular plate<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Sequence Analysis:**<br>- The protein begins with a hydrophobic signal peptide (MKMPLTWYSWFLLSAWILNTGAE), indicating it is a membrane-bound or secreted protein.<br>- The sequence contains multiple cadherin-like domains, identifiable by conserved motifs such as &quot;GPAHTGRE,&quot; &quot;VHGLSGELLAYNWYAG,&quot; and &quot;VGYGHMQVYEILAPPT.&quot;<br>- These features are characteristic of **Cadherin 23 (CDH23)**, a large transmembrane protein with multiple cadherin repeats.<br><br>**Biological Context:**<br>- CDH23 is a critical component of the mechanotransduction apparatus in cochlear hair cells.<br>- CDH23 specifically localizes to the **tips of stereocilia**, where it forms the upper end of the tip-link complex in conjunction with Protocadherin 15 (PCDH15).<br>- Tip links are the fine filamentous connections between the tip of one stereocilium and the side of the adjacent taller stereocilium, essential for mechanotransduction.<br><br>**Evaluating Choices:**<br>- A. Kinocilium tip — Not the primary localization site for CDH23.<br>- B. Hair-cell synaptic ribbon — Located at the basal end of hair cells; unrelated to CDH23 function.<br>- C. Stereocilium tip — Correct. CDH23 is a well-established component of the tip-link at the stereocilia tip.<br>- D. Hair-cell cuticular plate — Located at the base of stereocilia; not where CDH23 resides.<br><br>2. answer: C</td>

The question asks: &quot;which number of membrane-spanning helices is best supported when you assess hydrophobic and polar residue patterns rather than treating every hydrophobic patch as a separate span?&quot;

This phrasing suggests:
1. There are more hydrophobic patches than actual TMs
2. We should identify the &quot;true&quot; TMs based on proper hydrophobicity AND length
3. We should exclude short hydrophobic patches that don&#x27;t meet the criteria for a TM

The key criteria for a TM:
- Length: typically 18-25 hydrophobic residues
- Composition: predominantly hydrophobic (&gt;60-70% hydrophobic)
- Not too many charged residues within the segment

With 291 residues total, let me see what fits:

If 8 TMs: 8 × 20 = 160 in TMs, leaving 131 for loops (avg 16.4 per loop) - reasonable
If 9 TMs: 9 × 20 = 180 in TMs, leaving 111 for loops (avg 12.3 per loop) - tight but possible
If 10 TMs: 10 × 20 = 200 in TMs, leaving 91 for loops (avg 9.1 per loop) - very tight
If 11 TMs: 11 × 20 = 220 in TMs, leaving 71 for loops (avg 6.5 per loop) - too tight for protein folding

So 8 or 9 seems most reasonable from a structural standpoint.
